<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Find three prime numbers that add up to another prime number.

$

&= 10 + 13 \\\\
&= 23
\end{aligned}
$

You may use any of the signs $+, -, \times, \div$ and you can use brackets if you wish. 

</div>

**Question**

In the multiplication $6 \times 2 = 3$ all the digits are correct, but they are in the wrong places. The sum should be $2 \times 3 = 6$ or $(3 \times 2 = 6)$.

Another example is $3 \times 16 = 8$. This is wrong. It should be $3 \times 6 = 18$.

In the multiplications below the digits are also correct but in the wrong places. Write them again in the correct way:

<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

$21 \times 6 + 9 \times 6 = \ldots \times 6$ 

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$21 \times 6 + 9 \times 6 = \ldots \times 6$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
21 \times 6 + 9 \times 6   &= a \times 6       \\\\
6 \times (21 + 9)        &= a \times 6       \\\\
6 \times 30               &= a \times 6       \\\\
a &= \dfrac {6 \times 30} {6} \\\\
a &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 30} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a                          &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$30$

</div>

**Question**

$17 \times 14 + 16 \times 7 = \ldots \times 7 + \ldots \times 7$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$17 \times 14 + 16 \times 7 = \ldots \times 7 + \ldots \times 7$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<div class="tip">

</div>

%empty%
</div>

<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Let the missing number $= a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
17 \times 14 + 16 \times 7                &=  a \times 7 + a \times 7                       \\\\
238 + 112                                 &=  7a + 7a                                       \\\\
350                                       &=  14a                                           \\\\
a                                         &= \dfrac {350} {14}                              \\\\
a                                         &= \dfrac {7 \times 2 \times 25} {7 \times 2}                     \\\\
a                                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {7} \times \cancel {2} \times 25 } {\cancel {7} \times \cancel {2}  } \\\\
a                                         &= 25
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$25$

</div>

$

a           &=  72
\end{aligned}
$

Let the second missing number $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
17 + a      &=  32  \\\\
a           &=  32 - 17  \\\\
a           &=  15 \\\\
b           &= 32 - 39 \\\\
b           &=  -7
\end{aligned}
$
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Sam has the six number cards shown below.

$\Huge{\boxed{6} \ \boxed{5} \ \boxed{7} \ \boxed{2} \ \boxed{1} \ \boxed{8}}$

The cards can be placed together to form different numbers.

For example, using just five of the cards, the largest $5$-digit number that can be made is $87652$.

$\Huge{\boxed{8} \ \boxed{7} \ \boxed{6} \ \boxed{5} \ \boxed{2}}$

</div>

**Question**

What is the length of the crayon in millimetres?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">

</div>

<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 8.3 \ \text{centimetres} \\\\
&= 8.3 \times 10 \ \text{millimetres} \\\\
&= 83 \ \text{millimetres}
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>

<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

$83 \ \text{millimetres}$

**Question**

Jasmine has the six number cards shown below.

$\Huge{\boxed{1} \ \boxed{9} \ \boxed{5} \ \boxed{3} \ \boxed{8} \ \boxed{4}}$

The cards can be placed side by side to form different numbers.

For example, using four of the cards, the largest $4$-digit even number that can be made is $9854$.

<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Using five cards, what is the nearest number to $50000$ that can be made?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Using five cards, what is the nearest number to $50000$ that can be made?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

To be close to $50000$, the number should start with $4$ (approaching from below) or $5$ (approaching from above).

For the number closest to $50000$ starting with $4$, we want the largest possible value, so $49853$. The difference is $50000 - 49853 = 147$.

For the number closest to $50000$ starting with $5$, we want the smallest possible value, so $51348$. The difference is $51348 - 50000 = 1348$.

$147$ is smaller than $1348$, so $49853$ is the nearest.

Answer $= 49853$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. 

| Number 	| Difference 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|
| $49853$ 	| $50000 - 49853 = 147$ 	|
| $51348$ 	| $51348 - 50000 = 1348$ 	|

Nearest number $= 49853$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$49853$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Cameron has five number cards.

$\Large{\boxed{1} \quad \boxed{2} \quad \boxed{3} \quad \boxed{4} \quad \boxed{5}}$

The cards can be placed together to form a number.

For example, using three of his cards Cameron can create the smallest $3$-digit multiple of $3$.

$\Large{\boxed{1} \quad \boxed{2} \quad \boxed{3}}$

In the questions that follow, *choosing from Cameron's cards*, write numbers on the blank cards to make:

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<div class="question_envelope rag_not_found subquestion">
<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the remainder when $123456$ is divided by $5?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the remainder when $123456$ is divided by $5?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

To find the remainder when a number is divided by $5$, we just need to look at the digit in the units place.

The units digit of $123456$ is $6$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 \div 5 \\\\
&= 1 \ \text {remainder} \ 1
\end{aligned}
$

Remainder $= 1$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$1$

</div>
</div>

<li class="questionContainer">
<div class="question_envelope rag_not_found subquestion">
<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Calculate 
$7 \times 451 + 2 \times 451 + 451$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Calculate 
$7 \times 451 + 2 \times 451 + 451$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
- ABBR: Using BIDMAS
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 \times 451 + 2 \times 451 + 451 \\\\
&= 451 \times (7 + 2 + 1) \\\\
&= 451 \times 10 \\\\
&= 4510
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$4510$

</div>
</div>

</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">- rtq_abbr_note_md_bidmas
</code></pre>

<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$

</div>
</div>

<li class="questionContainer">
<div class="question_envelope rag_not_found subquestion">
<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

The digits $1,2,3,4,5$ and $6$ put into the following 
boxes to make two $3$ digit numbers and the second $3$ digit 
number is taken away from the first to give a total.

Each digit from $1$ to $6$ is used exactly once.

$
\begin{array}{ccccccc}
   & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} \\ \\
 {}-    & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} \\ \\
  \hline
  & & &  \\ \\
  \hline
\end{array}   
$

What is the largest total that can be made?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">The digits $1,2,3,4,5$ and $6$ put into the following 
boxes to make two $3$ digit numbers and the second $3$ digit 
number is taken away from the first to give a total.
<emptyline>
Each digit from $1$ to $6$ is used exactly once.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{ccccccc}
   & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} \\ \\
 {}-    & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} & \boxed{\phantom{55}} \\ \\
  \hline
  & & &  \\ \\
  \hline
\end{array}   
$
<emptyline>
What is the largest total that can be made?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The largest difference} &= 654 - 321 \\\\
                              &= 333
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
